> AGENT

I looked through it. There are two related layers:

**1. Actual Discord gateway**
The live Discord adapter is in [discord.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/agent/gateway/platforms/discord.py:408). It uses `discord.py`, creates a `commands.Bot`, enables message/member/voice intents, resolves `DISCORD_ALLOWED_USERS`, registers slash commands, then forwards messages into the generic gateway runner.

Flow:
1. `GatewayRunner.start()` creates enabled platform adapters and wires `adapter.set_message_handler(self._handle_message)` in [run.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/agent/gateway/run.py:977).
2. Discord `on_message` filters bot/system messages, then calls `DiscordAdapter._handle_message`.
3. In server channels, Discord requires an `@mention` unless configured as a free-response channel or already inside a participated thread. It can auto-create a thread for mentioned messages. See [discord.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/agent/gateway/platforms/discord.py:1893).
4. It normalizes Discord messages into `MessageEvent` with `source`, `message_id`, `media_urls`, attachment types, etc., then calls the base adapter pipeline at [discord.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/agent/gateway/platforms/discord.py:2098).
5. `GatewayRunner._handle_message()` checks authorization, handles slash commands, manages interrupts/queues, creates session context, runs the agent, persists transcript, and returns a response. See [run.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/agent/gateway/run.py:1507).
6. The base adapter sends the returned response through `DiscordAdapter.send()`, which fetches the channel, splits at Discord’s 2000-char limit, replies to the original message when possible, and falls back if replying to a system message fails. See [discord.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/agent/gateway/platforms/discord.py:626).

It also supports Discord slash commands by converting interactions into the same `MessageEvent` path. Slash commands include `reset`, `model`, `voice`, `thread`, `approve`, `deny`, etc. See [discord.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/agent/gateway/platforms/discord.py:1473).

**2. Megaplan/Arnold control plane**
The Sprint 6 “Discord functionality” for Arnold is deliberately Discord-independent inside megaplan. [control.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/control.py:1) says Arnold or any client writes `ControlMessage` rows, then megaplan claims and processes them.

Supported intents are `run_sprint`, `resume_plan`, `approve_gate`, and `reject_gate` in [control.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/control.py:35).

Flow:
1. Discord/Arnold side writes a `control_messages` row.
2. `process_pending_control_messages()` claims unclaimed messages.
3. Resolver validates `project_root`, epic, sprint, plan, or gate target.
4. Handler runs the right megaplan action:
   - `run_sprint` creates/resumes a sprint plan and calls `drive_auto`.
   - `resume_plan` calls `resume_plan`, optionally auto-continues.
   - `approve_gate` calls override `force-proceed`, emits `gate_resolved`, optionally continues.
   - `reject_gate` adds a user note and emits `gate_resolved`.

Progress back to Discord is via `progress_events`, emitted by [progress.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/progress.py:108). The control handlers pass progress context through env vars so subprocesses can keep writing events.

Important caveat: [control.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/control.py:7) explicitly notes claimed-but-unprocessed control messages can strand if the processor dies after claim and before marking processed. No stale-claim retry is implemented here yet.

So in short: Discord chat itself is handled by the vendored Hermes gateway; megaplan’s own Discord integration point is a database control/progress protocol that lets Arnold remain a thin Discord client.

> AGENT

I found the pattern. `Documents/Veas` is basically the model you want.

Veas runs one resident service: [app/main.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas/app/main.py:1). On startup it:
- opens the DB pool,
- starts Discord or WhatsApp transport,
- starts recovery,
- starts a scheduled-job worker,
- coalesces inbound chat bursts,
- turns due scheduled jobs back into agent turns.

The important part is the scheduler: [scheduled_jobs.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas/app/services/scheduled_jobs.py:1). It polls `scheduled_jobs`, claims due rows with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, dispatches a handler, marks fired, and retries/cancels failures. The agent itself has tools like `schedule_task`, `update_scheduled_task`, `cancel_scheduled_task`, and `list_scheduled_tasks` in [tool_schemas.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas/tool_schemas.py:1000). So the agent can say, “I should check back in an hour,” but a deterministic worker actually wakes up and does it.

Megaplan cloud is currently a CLI/provider layer, not a resident chat agent. The cloud CLI is in [megaplan/cloud/cli.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/cloud/cli.py:1). It loads `cloud.yaml`, gets a provider, and calls operations like:
- `cloud build`
- `cloud deploy`
- `cloud chain`
- `cloud bootstrap`
- `cloud status`
- `cloud logs`
- `cloud attach`
- `cloud exec`
- `cloud resume`

The provider abstraction is in [base.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/cloud/providers/base.py:1). Railway is a thin wrapper around Railway CLI SSH/logs/variables/deploy in [railway.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/cloud/providers/railway.py:1). The remote runner itself is a container with tmux sessions, a health server, a heartbeat watchdog, and wrappers like `mp-supervise` and `mp-heartbeat`. See [entrypoint.sh.tmpl](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/cloud/templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl:1) and [mp-heartbeat](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/cloud/wrappers/mp-heartbeat:1).

So the clean design is:

**Discord resident agent = operator/control plane. Cloud machine = execution plane.**

The Discord agent should not “be” the cloud runner. It should have a small set of tools that call the same cloud primitives megaplan already exposes.

A sensible tool surface would be:

- `cloud_status(plan?: str, chain?: bool)`  
  Calls `megaplan cloud status` or `cloud status --chain`, returns state, next step, current milestone, blocked/failed/terminal info.

- `cloud_logs(lines?: int, follow?: false)`  
  Fetches recent redacted logs. Probably no raw follow mode inside Discord.

- `cloud_start_chain(spec_id | uploaded_spec, idea_dir?)`  
  Stages a chain spec and starts remote `megaplan chain start`.

- `cloud_bootstrap(idea_text | idea_file, plan_name?, robustness?)`  
  Starts a new remote plan.

- `cloud_resume(plan?: str)`  
  Calls current `cloud resume`, which reads remote status, picks `next_step`, and runs it.

- `cloud_exec_safe(command)`  
  Optional and tightly gated. Better to expose specific actions first, not arbitrary shell.

- `schedule_cloud_check(run_id, interval, condition, notify_policy)`  
  Writes a DB row saying “check this cloud run every hour until terminal.”

- `cancel_cloud_check(run_id)` / `list_cloud_checks()`.

Then the runtime loop would work like Veas:

1. You DM Discord: “Run the Arnold chain on the cloud box and keep an eye on it.”
2. Agent calls `cloud_start_chain(...)`.
3. Agent calls `schedule_cloud_check(interval=1 hour, condition=until_terminal)`.
4. Scheduler wakes every N seconds, claims due checks.
5. Check handler calls `cloud_status --chain`, maybe `cloud_logs --no-follow`.
6. Deterministic rules decide:
   - running normally: maybe stay quiet,
   - stalled: notify you,
   - blocked/awaiting human: summarize and ask you,
   - failed: summarize logs and suggest/retry,
   - next step available and policy says auto-continue: call `cloud_resume`,
   - terminal: send final summary and cancel recurrence.

The agent can still reason conversationally, but the recurring wakeups should be boring infrastructure, not an LLM loop that hopes it remembers to wake up.

There are two implementation paths:

**Fast path:** add Hermes/Discord tools that shell out to `megaplan cloud ...`, plus a simple scheduled-task table/worker copied from the Veas pattern. This is enough for “talk to Discord, run cloud things, check hourly, report back.”

**Stronger path:** reuse megaplan’s existing `control_messages` / `progress_events` idea. Discord writes durable cloud-control rows, a local/resident processor claims them, and cloud progress flows back as events. That gives better auditability and avoids turning Discord into a pile of shell commands.

My recommendation: implement a small `cloud_runs` + `cloud_watch_jobs` layer, backed by Veas-style scheduling, with tools wrapping the existing provider API directly rather than parsing CLI output. Use CLI-compatible behavior at the boundary, but call Python functions internally where possible. That gets you a Discord operator that can start work, check on work, resume work, and talk to you when human judgment is needed.

> DEVELOPER

And would this save the plans and run off the database then? It's just like helping. First of all, is there anything we should take from the other repo? So let me just think that it's true. So basically we talk to the agents, then how does that agent work with the Epic creation tool? and I guess it just triggers plans in cloud mode and then they execute and then it checks in on them. Is that correct? But when we're working in epic mode, does it just go back and forth between, yeah, like to the questions with us? Is that correct or how does it work?

> AGENT

I’m going to check the epic/editorial command surface so I can answer the “epic mode back-and-forth” part against the actual megaplan code, not just the design docs.

> AGENT

Yes, that’s basically the shape, with one caveat: today megaplan cloud still executes primarily from a remote filesystem workspace, not purely from the DB.

Current behavior:
- Epics can live in `FileStore`, `DBStore`, or `MultiStore`.
- Cloud runs clone/use a repo at `repo.workspace`, then create/read plan dirs under `.megaplan/plans/...` on the cloud machine’s persistent volume.
- `control_messages` and `progress_events` are DB-ready, but they are more of a control/progress bridge than a full “plans execute entirely from DB” runtime.
- `PlanRepository` still intentionally works against real plan dirs because workers and artifacts expect files.

So if we did this now, I’d expect:
1. Discord agent stores epic state in DB.
2. When a sprint/plan needs execution, it triggers a cloud run.
3. Cloud run writes plan artifacts on the cloud volume.
4. Status/progress gets mirrored back via `progress_events`, and optionally snapshots/artifacts can be copied into DB after phase/run completion.

That is probably the right first version. Full DB-native execution can come later.

**What To Take From Veas**
I would take the architecture, not copy the domain logic.

Useful pieces:
- DB-backed `scheduled_jobs` table pattern.
- `ScheduledJobWorker` claim loop with `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`.
- Startup recovery for stale claimed jobs.
- Tool surface for `schedule_task`, `update_scheduled_task`, `cancel_scheduled_task`, `list_scheduled_tasks`.
- Burst coalescing for chat messages.
- The split between user-facing response phase and state-write phase.

I would not bring over the mediation-specific prompts, OOB logic, partner concepts, or relationship data model.

**How Epic Mode Would Work**
You talk to the Discord agent. It has tools roughly like:

- `create_epic(title, goal, initial_body)`
- `select_epic(...)`
- `read_epic(...)`
- `edit_epic_body(...)`
- `add_checklist_items(...)`
- `create_sprint(...)`
- `queue_sprints(...)`
- `mark_epic_planned(...)`
- `run_sprint_on_cloud(...)`
- `cloud_status(...)`
- `schedule_cloud_check(...)`

In shaping mode, yes, it goes back and forth with you. But the back-and-forth is just normal conversation plus tool calls. The agent asks clarifying questions when needed, then writes decisions into the epic body/checklist/sprints. It does not need to run cloud execution yet.

Once the epic has queued sprints, the agent can trigger cloud execution:
- “Run sprint 2” creates a `ControlMessage` or directly calls a cloud tool.
- The cloud machine starts/resumes a megaplan plan.
- The scheduler checks hourly or every N minutes.
- If status is normal, it can stay quiet.
- If blocked, failed, waiting for approval, or complete, it messages you.

So your mental model is right:

`Discord conversation -> epic tools update DB -> sprint/plan trigger -> cloud runner executes -> scheduled monitor checks cloud -> Discord agent reports or asks you`

The main design decision is whether the Discord agent directly shells out to `megaplan cloud ...`, or whether it writes durable DB control rows and a processor performs the cloud operations. I’d choose durable DB control rows for anything long-running, because it gives recovery, auditability, and less “the chat process was holding everything together.”

> DEVELOPER

Do you think we could make it so this whole repo is basically wrapped by VEA, you know? So that kind of acts as like the agent orchestrator and then, you know, we have like a single disk bot for existing functionality. And for this...

> AGENT

Yes. I think that is the right direction, with one important framing:

Veas should not literally wrap all of megaplan internals. It should become the **resident chat/orchestration shell**, and megaplan stays the **planning/execution engine**.

That gives you:

`Discord bot / scheduler / DB / audit / reminders / human conversation` from Veas-style runtime

plus

`epics / plans / reviews / gates / cloud runners / execution` from megaplan.

The clean shape would be:

**Resident Orchestrator**
A long-running service, Veas-like:
- owns Discord connection
- stores conversations, bot turns, tool calls, scheduled jobs
- coalesces message bursts
- runs recovery on startup
- has a scheduler that checks cloud jobs
- sends proactive messages

**Megaplan Engine**
Used through tools/API calls:
- create/read/update epics
- manage checklist and sprint queue
- start a plan
- run sprint on cloud
- resume plan
- inspect status
- approve/reject gates
- fetch logs/progress

So the Discord agent becomes the front door for both:
- “Help me shape this epic”
- “Turn that into sprints”
- “Run sprint 2 on the cloud box”
- “Check every hour and tell me if it’s stuck”
- “Approve this gate”
- “What happened overnight?”

I’d avoid merging the repos wholesale at first. Better path:

1. **Extract the Veas runtime pattern**
   Bring over scheduler, DB job claiming, recovery, Discord burst handling, and tool-call audit concepts.

2. **Add a megaplan bot profile**
   Similar to Veas’ `MediatorBotSpec`, but instead of mediation tools it gets `megaplan_tools`.

3. **Expose megaplan tools**
   Start with:
   - `create_epic`
   - `read_epic`
   - `edit_epic_body`
   - `list_sprints`
   - `create_or_update_sprints`
   - `run_sprint_cloud`
   - `cloud_status`
   - `cloud_resume`
   - `schedule_cloud_check`

4. **Use DB as orchestration truth**
   Conversations, scheduled checks, tool calls, control messages, and progress events live in DB.
   Plan artifacts can initially remain on disk/cloud volume, with snapshots mirrored back as needed.

5. **Only later make plans fully DB-native**
   That is a larger change because current workers still expect real plan directories.

The end result would feel like “one Discord bot for everything,” but architecturally it’s a resident operator calling a planning engine and cloud runner.

I would call it something like:

`resident_megaplan_runtime`
or
`megaplan_resident`

and let it reuse Veas’ best ideas without importing the relationship-mediator domain.

> DEVELOPER

Is there a shared base both repos should work off of?

> AGENT

Yes. The shared base should be a small **resident agent runtime**, not “Veas” and not “megaplan” itself.

Think of three layers:

**1. Shared Runtime Base**
A reusable package both repos can use:
- Discord/transport adapter
- inbound message persistence
- burst coalescing
- bot turn records
- tool call audit
- outbound sending
- scheduled jobs
- recovery/stale-claim handling
- basic admin/health endpoints
- bot profile interface
- tool registry interface

This is the part Veas has already proven.

**2. Domain Bot Profile**
Each product supplies its own profile:
- Veas profile: mediation prompt, mediation tools, partner/check-in/OOB rules.
- Megaplan profile: epic shaping prompt, editorial tools, cloud control tools, gate approval rules.

**3. Domain Engine**
Each product keeps its real business logic:
- Veas keeps mediation models/services.
- Megaplan keeps `Store`, editorial APIs, plan lifecycle, cloud providers, workers, control messages, progress events.

So the shared base might look like:

```text
resident_runtime/
  transports/
    discord.py
  scheduler.py
  coalescing.py
  turns.py
  tool_registry.py
  outbound.py
  recovery.py
  admin.py
  bots.py
```

Then:

```text
veas/
  bot_profile.py
  tools/
  services/
```

```text
megaplan_resident/
  bot_profile.py
  tools/
    epic_tools.py
    cloud_tools.py
    control_tools.py
```

The key design rule: the shared base should know nothing about epics, mediation, sprints, partners, gates, or cloud providers. It only knows how to run a resident chat agent reliably.

I would not start by extracting a perfect library. I’d first create the megaplan resident runtime by copying the minimum proven Veas patterns, then extract the common base once the second use case proves the boundary. That avoids abstracting around the wrong seams.

  "patch": "*** Begin Patch\n*** Add File: .megaplan/ideas/resident-discord-cloud-orchestrator.md\n+# Resident Discord Cloud Orchestrator for Megaplan\n+\n+Build a resident Discord-facing orchestration layer into this megaplan repo, using the proven architectural patterns from `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas` while keeping megaplan as the planning/execution engine.\n+\n+## Goal\n+\n+Create a single Discord bot surface that can:\n+\n+- Talk with the user to shape epics.\n+- Persist epic/conversation/orchestration state durably.\n+- Use megaplan editorial APIs to create, read, and update epics, bodies, checklists, and sprints.\n+- Trigger sprint/plan execution on megaplan cloud runners.\n+- Check in on cloud runs periodically.\n+- Resume, inspect, or report on cloud work based on conversation and scheduled monitoring.\n+- Ask the user when human input is needed, such as gate approval, failed runs, blocked runs, or ambiguous epic-shaping questions.\n+\n+The result should feel like a resident operator: the user can talk naturally in Discord, the bot maintains state, starts cloud work, checks on it, and reports back without requiring the user to keep a local terminal open.\n+\n+## Settled Decisions\n+\n+- **SD-001** — Megaplan remains the planning and execution engine; the new layer is a resident chat/orchestration shell. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: Megaplan already owns plan lifecycle, editorial APIs, cloud provider commands, control messages, and progress events. The resident layer should coordinate those capabilities, not duplicate or replace them.\n+\n+- **SD-002** — Take architectural patterns from Veas, not Veas' mediation domain logic. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: Veas has useful resident-service patterns: Discord ingestion, burst coalescing, bot turn/tool-call audit, DB-backed scheduled jobs, stale-claim recovery, health/admin surfaces, and phase separation. Its relationship-mediation prompts, partner model, OOB rules, and domain tables should not be imported.\n+\n+- **SD-003** — DB is the orchestration truth for conversations, bot turns, tool calls, scheduled checks, control messages, and progress events. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: Long-running chat/cloud orchestration must survive process restarts and support audit/recovery. Scheduled monitoring and cloud-trigger commands should be durable rows, not in-memory state.\n+\n+- **SD-004** — Plan execution can initially remain filesystem/cloud-volume based; do not force full DB-native plan execution in this feature. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: Current `PlanRepository`, workers, artifacts, and cloud runners expect real plan directories under `.megaplan/plans`. Moving all plan artifacts into DB is a larger migration and should not block the resident orchestrator.\n+\n+- **SD-005** — Mirror or summarize cloud progress back into DB through existing progress/control concepts where practical. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: The Discord agent needs a durable, queryable view of remote state. Cloud plan artifacts may remain remote, but status/progress, run metadata, and important outcomes should be reflected in DB tables or store-backed events.\n+\n+- **SD-006** — Expose cloud operations as constrained tools, not as arbitrary shell by default. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: The bot should have tools such as `cloud_status`, `cloud_start_chain`, `cloud_bootstrap`, `cloud_resume`, `cloud_logs`, and `schedule_cloud_check`. Arbitrary remote command execution should be gated or omitted from the first version.\n+\n+- **SD-007** — Scheduled cloud check-ins should be deterministic infrastructure. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: The agent can decide to schedule a check, but a worker should claim due jobs and run status checks. The LLM should not be responsible for remembering timers.\n+\n+- **SD-008** — Preserve a clean boundary between shared resident runtime, megaplan bot profile/tools, and megaplan engine. _load_bearing: true_\n+  Rationale: The eventual shared base should know about transports, scheduling, turns, tool calls, recovery, and outbound delivery, but not epics, mediation, sprints, partners, gates, or cloud providers.\n+\n+## Reference Code To Inspect\n+\n+From Veas:\n+\n+- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas/app/main.py`\n+- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas/app/services/scheduled_jobs.py`\n+- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas/app/services/scheduled_job_handlers.py`\n+- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas/app/bots/mediator.py`\n+- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas/tool_schemas.py`, especially scheduled-task schemas\n+- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/Veas/resident_chat_runtime/*`, especially Discord/coalescing/runtime pieces\n+\n+From megaplan:\n+\n+- `megaplan/agent/gateway/platforms/discord.py`\n+- `megaplan/agent/gateway/run.py`\n+- `megaplan/control.py`\n+- `megaplan/progress.py`\n+- `megaplan/editorial/*`\n+- `megaplan/store/*`\n+- `megaplan/cloud/cli.py`\n+- `megaplan/cloud/providers/*`\n+- `megaplan/cloud/templates/entrypoint.sh.tmpl`\n+- `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/mp-supervise`\n+- `megaplan/cloud/wrappers/mp-heartbeat`\n+- `docs/cloud.md`\n+\n+## Desired Architecture\n+\n+Add a resident megaplan runtime inside this repo, likely under a new package such as `megaplan/resident/` or `megaplan/agent/resident_megaplan/`, with a small reusable runtime boundary and a megaplan-specific bot profile.\n+\n+Conceptual layers:\n+\n+1. Resident runtime:\n+   - Discord transport and/or adapter reuse.\n+   - Message persistence.\n+   - Burst coalescing.\n+   - Bot turn and tool-call audit.\n+   - Outbound delivery.\n+   - Scheduled jobs.\n+   - Startup recovery for stale claimed jobs.\n+   - Health/admin surfaces if compatible with existing repo style.\n+\n+2. Megaplan bot profile:\n+   - System prompt/instructions for epic shaping and cloud orchestration.\n+   - Tool registry for safe megaplan operations.\n+   - Conversation behavior: ask clarifying questions in shaping mode; use tools to persist decisions; trigger cloud only when an executable sprint/plan exists or the user asks.\n+\n+3. Megaplan tools:\n+   - Epic tools:\n+     - `create_epic`\n+     - `select_epic`\n+     - `read_epic`\n+     - `edit_epic_body`\n+     - `add_checklist_items`\n+     - `update_checklist_item`\n+     - `create_or_update_sprints`\n+     - `queue_sprints`\n+     - `transition_epic_state`\n+   - Cloud tools:\n+     - `cloud_status`\n+     - `cloud_status_chain`\n+     - `cloud_start_chain`\n+     - `cloud_bootstrap`\n+     - `cloud_resume`\n+     - `cloud_logs`\n+     - `schedule_cloud_check`\n+     - `cancel_cloud_check`\n+     - `list_cloud_checks`\n+   - Gate/control tools:\n+     - `approve_gate`\n+     - `reject_gate`\n+     - `run_sprint_on_cloud`\n+\n+## Runtime Flow\n+\n+Epic shaping flow:\n+\n+1. User messages Discord.\n+2. Resident runtime coalesces bursts.\n+3. Megaplan bot profile loads hot context: active epic, recent messages, open questions, checklist, sprints, cloud watches.\n+4. Agent asks clarifying questions when the user intent is ambiguous.\n+5. Agent writes stable decisions into epic body/checklist/sprints through editorial/store APIs.\n+6. When the epic reaches planned/queued sprint readiness, the bot can offer or accept commands to run a sprint.\n+\n+Cloud run flow:\n+\n+1. User asks to run a sprint, plan, or chain.\n+2. Bot validates an executable target from DB/store state.\n+3. Bot writes a durable control/run record and starts cloud work through provider-backed cloud APIs or the existing cloud CLI behavior.\n+4. Bot schedules a cloud check job if the work is long-running.\n+5. Scheduler claims due check jobs, calls cloud status/log tools, updates DB, and decides whether to stay silent, notify, resume, or ask the user.\n+6. On blocked/failed/gate-needed/completed states, Discord receives a concise status message with actionable options.\n+\n+## What Should Not Happen\n+\n+- Do not copy Veas mediation prompts, partner tables, OOB checks, or relationship-specific logic.\n+- Do not require plans to execute fully from DB in the first implementation.\n+- Do not make the Discord agent depend on an in-memory timer for check-ins.\n+- Do not expose unrestricted remote shell execution as the main cloud interface.\n+- Do not break existing `megaplan cloud` CLI workflows.\n+- Do not break existing local file-backed plan workflows.\n+\n+## Implementation Expectations\n+\n+This is a cross-cutting feature. The plan should be careful and staged:\n+\n+1. Identify the minimal resident runtime pieces to build or reuse from existing `megaplan/agent/gateway`.\n+2. Define any new DB/store models needed for scheduled jobs, resident bot turns, cloud run records, and cloud watch jobs.\n+3. Add a scheduler with stale-claim recovery similar to Veas.\n+4. Add a megaplan tool registry/profile that calls existing editorial, control, progress, and cloud provider code.\n+5. Add tests for store/model behavior, scheduler claiming/recovery, tool wrappers, and cloud status/check decisions.\n+6. Keep the initial cloud execution artifact path filesystem-based, but persist run metadata and progress summaries.\n+\n+## Success Criteria\n+\n+- A Discord resident runtime can accept a user message and dispatch it through a megaplan-specific bot profile without importing Veas domain code.\n+- The bot profile has a defined and tested tool surface for epic shaping and cloud orchestration.\n+- A DB/store-backed scheduled job worker can claim due cloud check jobs safely, including stale-claim recovery.\n+- Cloud checks can inspect current cloud status/chain status and classify at least: running, blocked, failed, gate-needed, completed, and unknown.\n+- A cloud run/check record is durable and recoverable after process restart.\n+- Existing `megaplan cloud` commands and existing local plan workflows continue to pass focused regression tests.\n+- The plan explicitly preserves the filesystem/cloud-volume plan execution model for the first version while leaving room for later DB-native artifacts.\n*** End Patch\n"
}

__init__(self, token: str) -> None:\n        self.token = token\n\n    async def send(self, message: OutboundMessage) -> None:\n        await asyncio.to_thread(self._send_sync, message.content)\n\n    def _request(self, url: str, payload: dict[str, object]) -> dict[str, object]:\n        body = json.dumps(payload).encode('utf-8')\n        req = urllib.request.Request(\n            url,\n event = InboundEvent(\n        idempotency_key=f'discord:message:{MESSAGE_ID}',\n        conversation_key=f'discord:dm:{USER_ID}',\n        subject=AuthorizationSubject(user_id=USER_ID, guild_id=None, channel_id=CHANNEL_ID),\n        content=CONTENT,\n        raw={'discord_message_id': MESSAGE_ID, 'dm_user_id': USER_ID},\n    )\n    await runtime.receive(event)\n    await asyncio.sleep(config.burst_idle_delay_s + 3)\n    store.close()\n    print('replayed_latest_message')\n\ntry:\n    asyncio.run(main())\nexcept Exception:\n    traceback.print_exc()\n    raise\nPY",

`python -m megaplan resident discord --mode production`

It did receive your message. The actual bug was that Arnold’s existing Discord ingestion had already inserted the same Discord message into `messages`, so the resident bot hit the unique constraint on `discord_message_id` and failed before replying.

I patched `DBStore` to adopt an existing Discord message row instead of trying to insert a duplicate, pushed it to `main`:

`11a71c81 Adopt existing Discord messages in DBStore`

I also replayed your latest `hey` through the resident path, and the DB now shows the turn as `completed` with `message_sent: true`. The live bot is still connected, so the next Discord message should go through normally.

> AGENT

Yes, for the resident Discord bot in production, it is using the database path:

`MEGAPLAN_BACKEND=db`
`DBStore(actor_id="resident")`

So Discord conversations, inbound/outbound messages, resident turns, tool calls, epics, sprints, checklist edits, plan records, plan artifacts, cloud runs, scheduled cloud checks, progress events, control messages, and confirmations are intended to persist to the DB.

Cloud launch/check behavior exists too. The resident tools call the existing `megaplan cloud` CLI through `CloudCliBackend`, then persist:

- `cloud_runs`: one row per cloud operation
- `scheduled_jobs`: periodic `cloud_check` jobs
- `progress_events`: status changes like running/completed/failed/gate-needed
- `messages`: notifications back to Discord
- `bot_turns` / `tool_calls`: what the agent did and why

But I would not yet say “absolutely everything from existing Megaplan has a complete database equivalent” without qualification. The DB foundation exists for the main objects: `epics`, `sprints`, `messages`, `bot_turns`, `tool_calls`, `plans`, `plan_artifacts`, `execution_leases`, `migration_runs`, `control_messages`, `progress_events`, resident tables, and cloud tables. There is also a migration path for legacy local plans.

The likely remaining gap is storage policy for large/non-text artifacts. `plan_artifacts` has DB text/binary support, and the repo has blob abstractions including local and Supabase storage, but we should be deliberate:

- DB tables should store audit metadata, state, decisions, compact text artifacts, IDs, hashes, and references.
- Supabase Storage or another blob store should hold large logs, full stdout/stderr dumps, generated files, binary artifacts, images/audio, exported bundles, and maybe full cloud run worktree snapshots.
- DB rows should point to those blobs with content hash, size, content type, and provenance.

So: the resident agent can launch/check cloud work and it does save the operational trail to DB. The next hardening step is an audit/storage pass: enumerate every local `.megaplan` artifact and every cloud output, confirm whether it maps to a DB table or blob ref, then add missing blob persistence where needed.

> DEVELOPER

And what are the tools this thing likely to need? Does it have access to basically everything Megaplan can already do or is there stuff that's missing? Like for example, will it be able to search stuff, will it be able to reconcile stuff, will it be able to manually edit things? Will it be able to, you know, do kind of keyword search? Is it able to kind of like see all the repos that we're in? You know, does yeah, is they able to add a new repo? Yeah, all that kind of stuff.

> AGENT

I’ll check the resident tool catalog directly so I can answer what it can do today versus what would need to be added. I’ll also look at whether it exposes generic repo/file/search operations or only Megaplan-specific tools.

> AGENT

Today, no: the Discord resident agent does **not** have access to everything Megaplan can do. It has a deliberately constrained tool surface in [profile.py](/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/megaplan/megaplan/resident/profile.py:261).

What it has now:

- Epic workflow:
  `create_epic`, `select_epic`, `read_epic`, `edit_epic_body`
- Checklist/sprint editing:
  `add_checklist_items`, `update_checklist_item`, `create_or_update_sprints`, `queue_sprints`
- State/gate/control:
  `transition_epic_state`, `approve_gate`, `reject_gate`, `run_sprint_on_cloud`
- Cloud:
  `cloud_status`, `cloud_status_chain`, `cloud_start_chain`, `cloud_bootstrap`, `cloud_resume`, `cloud_logs`
- Scheduling:
  `schedule_cloud_check`, `cancel_cloud_check`, `list_cloud_checks`

So it can shape epics, manually edit epic bodies/checklists/sprints, launch/resume/check cloud work, read constrained logs, and schedule check-ins.

What it does **not** currently expose as tools:

- General keyword search over messages, epics, plans, code artifacts, or docs.
- Search/reconcile tools for finding duplicate epics, stale plans, orphaned runs, missing artifacts, etc.
- Repo discovery: list all repos/workspaces you are in.
- Add/register a new repo/codebase.
- GitHub repo search, PR/issue operations, branch/commit operations.
- File-system read/edit tools.
- Arbitrary shell/command execution.
- General Megaplan CLI access.
- Plan lifecycle tools like `megaplan init`, `plan`, `critique`, `execute`, `review`, except indirectly via constrained cloud commands.
- Direct plan artifact edit/read tools.
- Blob/storage archival tools.
- Codebase research/search tools, even though DB tables like `codebases` and `code_artifacts` exist.

The prompt explicitly says it should “not run arbitrary remote shell commands,” so this is currently designed as a safe operator, not a full Codex-style agent.

The next tool layer I’d add:

1. `search_messages`
2. `search_epics`
3. `search_plans`
4. `search_code_artifacts`
5. `list_codebases`
6. `register_codebase`
7. `reconcile_epic`
8. `reconcile_plan_storage`
9. `list_repos`
10. `add_repo`
11. `read_plan_artifact`
12. `write_plan_artifact`
13. `export_epic_bundle`
14. `archive_cloud_logs`
15. `open_megaplan_run` / `start_megaplan_plan`

I’d keep destructive or broad tools behind admin authorization and exact confirmation, same as cloud start. That gives it “basically Megaplan operator” power without turning Discord into arbitrary shell access.

> AGENT

Implemented and pushed.

Commit: `2d019232 Expand resident Discord operator tools`

The resident bot now has the expanded tool catalog: search messages/epics/plans/code artifacts, list/register repos/codebases, read/write plan artifacts, export epic bundles, reconcile epic/plan storage, archive cloud logs, plus the existing epic/checklist/sprint/cloud tools. It still does **not** expose arbitrary shell/exec or unrestricted filesystem editing.

Repo/cloud approach is now:

- Repos are registered as codebases with durable `repo_url`, `repo_workspace`, and branch metadata.
- Cloud start/bootstrap can resolve a registered repo/codebase.
- If the cloud machine workspace is missing `.git`, the cloud path performs an internal fixed clone-if-missing step before upload/exec.
- If `.git` already exists, it skips clone and reuses the existing workspace.
- This stays behind admin/confirmation boundaries; there is no Discord-facing “run git clone arbitrary shell” tool.

Verification:
- Focused suite: `99 passed`
- Compile/check: passed
- Megaplan review: `14/15 success criteria passed`, no issues/rework. The remaining item is manual Discord smoke testing.
- Full suite still has the same 5 known unrelated failures reported earlier.

Live bot has been restarted on the new code:

`PID 16953`
`python -m megaplan resident discord --mode production`

I also applied the live DB migration for `repo_url` / `repo_workspace`, and resident health is passing against `DBStore`.

Cleaned and pushed to `main`.

What changed in commit `b4cfe2b0`:
- Removed Arnold merge planning artifacts: `ideas/arnold-merge/`, `chain.yaml`, `cloud.yaml`, Arnold design/spike/audit docs, old `idea-*.txt`, `plan.md`, `planning-bot-spec.md`, and run output JSONs.
- Pruned the vendored Hermes subtree from ~120 MB to ~34 MB by removing docs/site/eval/generated-run dead weight while keeping required runtime files.
- Added `.gitignore` rules so those local/generated files do not get staged again.
- Left actual Megaplan source, resident Discord code, Supabase migrations, and Arnold schema/runtime code intact.

Verification:
- Vendored Hermes audit now reports no missing runtime entries and no dead weight.
- Focused cleanup/runtime tests passed: `44 passed`.
- Full suite ran: `1245 passed, 10 skipped, 2 failed`. The two failures are source-level behavior tests outside this cleanup scope:
  - `tests/test_checks.py::test_validate_critique_checks_rejects_light_mode_stray_checks`
  - `tests/test_init_plan.py::test_handle_plan_failure_clears_active_step`

`main` and `origin/main` are both at `b4cfe2b0`; working tree is clean.
